## 1. Family / fuzz-0 status
- Patch: `patches/.18.16.0-families/0102-omp-computer-control-confirm-deadline-v18.16.0.patch`, 2 hunks, 11 changed lines, one owned path `pi-coding-agent/src/tools/computer/supervisor.ts` (manifest.json:615-625).
- fuzz-0 on 18.18.0: **applies cleanly, 0 failing hunks**; replay log `/home/chris/.cache/omp-plan-18180/replay-logs/0102-omp-computer-control-confirm-deadline.txt` = `patching file src/tools/computer/supervisor.ts` only.
- Semantics re-verified, not assumed:
  - Upstream 18.18.0 still has `const deadline = AbortSignal.timeout(message.timeoutMs);` at `supervisor.ts:420`, `AbortSignal.any` at :421, verdict ternary `approved | deadline.aborted ? "unanswered" : "denied"` at :436 — byte-for-byte the pre-patch shape the hunk rewrites. `#confirmControl` still starts at :395, the `no-ui` guard at :411, the `timeoutMs <= 0` guard at :413-415.
  - `timeoutMs` producer unchanged: `worker.ts:1454` `deadline - Math.min(CONTROL_ANSWER_RESERVE_MS, message.timeoutMs / 4)` with `CONTROL_ANSWER_RESERVE_MS = 5_000` at `worker.ts:143` (identical text in 18.16.0 at worker.ts:1449/142).
  - `supervisor.ts` is unmodified 18.16.0→18.18.0: both trees list 27.1KB, and every probed anchor (13/14, 42-48, 51, 74, 82, 93, 100, 108, 110, 156-157, 183-187, 386-440, 537-541, 612-695) sits at the **identical line number** in `18.16.0/.../tools/computer/supervisor.ts` and `18.18.0/.../tools/computer/supervisor.ts`. No drifts, no upstream rewrite.
  - The cap is a pure `Math.min(message.timeoutMs, CONTROL_CONFIRM_UNANSWERED_MS)` on a locally created signal; it does not read any upstream-provided constant, so it survives any future `timeoutMs` formula change (it only ever shortens).

## 2. Per failing hunk
None. Both hunks (insert `CONTROL_CONFIRM_UNANSWERED_MS = 60_000` after `RESTART_MESSAGE`; wrap the timeout in `Math.min(..., CONTROL_CONFIRM_UNANSWERED_MS)`) apply at offset 0 with full context. Cause classification: n/a (context drift = none; upstream did not rewrite the logic; upstream does not already do it).

## 3. Capability classification
**0102 — KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL, effort S.**
- Not ADOPT-CANDIDATE: upstream 18.18.0 has no answer cap of its own — grep for `CONTROL_CONFIRM|CONFIRM_UNANSWERED|confirm.*60_000` over the whole 18.18.0 `src/` returns **no matches**, and the only deadline is the run-budget-derived `message.timeoutMs` (`supervisor.ts:420`). Consumer proof therefore cannot be manufactured: without our patch a 1-hour run keeps the dialog up to ~59m55s (the 5 s reserve), and the TUI `timeout` option cannot be used because a timed-out `HookSelectorComponent` selects the highlighted option (`pi-tui/src/overlays/hook-selector.ts:294-306`, `extension-ui-controller.ts:1222-1231` maps `"Yes"` → `true`) — i.e. timing out upstream way would *approve* foreground control, a real regression the patch prevents.
- Rebase method: replay the 2 hunks verbatim onto the 18.18.0 `supervisor.ts` (proven clean at fuzz-0), then re-pin the gate's tree assertion `assert.equal(version, "18.16.0")` at `patches/verify-computer-control-confirm-deadline.ts:39` to `"18.18.0"`. No code change.
- Owning gate: `verify-computer-control-confirm-deadline.ts` (0102), registered unconditionally at `patches/apply.sh:598`. Its four legs remain meaningful on 18.18.0: 60 s cap beating a 595 s run deadline (`:301-338`), run deadline winning when shorter (`:341-355`), answered/denied unchanged (`:358-388`), `timeoutMs<=0` short-circuit (`:390-400`), negative control that the real `HookSelectorComponent` with `{timeout}` selects `"Yes"` (`:404-421`).
- Portability note for the gate: its header comment cites `extension-ui-controller.ts:1225-1232`; in 18.18.0 `showHookConfirm` is at `:1222-1231`. Behaviour identical, so only the comment's line ref needs refreshing (cosmetic, not a gate failure — the gate imports `HookSelectorComponent`, not that file).
- Effort S: 2 clean hunks + one version-constant re-pin; no semantic work.

## 4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near the owned path that could silently break the invariant
- **`supervisor.ts` itself: none** (identical bytes/lines, see §1). This is the strongest result for this family.
- `tools/computer/window-miss.ts` 2.9KB→3.3KB: added `export function matchesWindowApp(window, app, pids?)` (`window-miss.ts:16-19`) — app-name/PID matching for app IDs, paths and Wayland permission timeouts (`CHANGELOG.md:203`). Severity to 0102: **none** — 0102's comment claims the script "goes on with window input", and window input routing did not change; only window *lookup* diagnostics did.
- `tools/computer/worker.ts` 74.4KB→75.7KB: the confirm machinery inside it (`#confirmControl` at :1682-1691, `answerDeadline` at :1687, reserve at :143) is textually unchanged; the growth is elsewhere (window/app handling, cf. `CHANGELOG.md:148` `computer` zoom, `:203`, `:973`). Severity **low**: if a future change moved `#confirmControl`, only `timeoutMs`'s value would move and the `min()` cap still holds.
- `CHANGELOG.md:644`/`:700` (`computer` runs in subagents and `omp -p` keep working through background window input instead of stopping at the first `control.acquire`) and `:899` (`no-ui`/`denied` reasons) are **pre-18.16** behaviour the patch already assumes; no 18.17/18.18 entry touches `control.acquire` (18.17.0 section = `CHANGELOG.md:87`, 18.18.0 = `:5`).
- No cache-prefix / compaction / OAuth / idle-request surface is involved: the family touches no prompt bytes, no auth, no timer that survives a turn. Its only cross-cutting risk is a hung human dialog, which is exactly what the cap bounds.

## 5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads` > 0)
**No — this capability does not rely on main-thread-only state.**
- Evidence: the cap is a per-instance, per-thread `AbortSignal.timeout` composed with the instance's own `AbortController` (`supervisor.ts:398-421`); `#pending` is an instance field keyed by runId (`:396`, `:438`). Nothing is read from a process-wide singleton to compute the deadline.
- The dialog branch is **unreachable for subagents in any thread**: subagent sessions are built with `hasUI: false` (`task/executor.ts:4963`, `task/persisted-revive.ts:486-487`, `thread/worker-warm-up.ts:61`), and `supervisor.ts:411-412` returns `no-ui` *before* the deadline branch, so a worker-hosted subagent never opens the confirm at all. Worker mode therefore cannot turn the 60 s cap into a hang or a crash.
- Concrete failure mode with `workerThreads>0`: none for 0102. The residual, pre-existing (not-0102) observation is that `ownedSupervisors`/`ownerTurns` maps (`supervisor.ts:613-690`) are per-thread module state, so a main-thread `revokeComputerControlForOwner`/`releaseComputerSessionsForOwner` for an owner whose agent session lives in a worker thread would not reach that worker's supervisors — silent (no crash), and it cannot hang a dialog because the worker never prompts. Worth one line in the plan's worker-mode notes; it is upstream behaviour, not a patch defect, and no 18.16 gate covers it.
- Gate leg that would catch a worker-mode regression of *this* family: `verify-computer-control-confirm-deadline.ts:301-338` (cap must fire at 60 s and the run must still be servable afterwards) plus `:404-421` (the "timed-out TUI confirm = Yes" negative control). Both are main-thread-only today; if the plan adds a worker-mode matrix, the honest addition for 0102 is a subagent-side assertion that the verdict is `no-ui` (not a 60 s wait) — a 5-line extra leg, no new gate.

**1. Status on 18.18.0**

Verified independently: the patch's two contexts match 18.18.0 byte-for-byte (`18.18.0/…/pi-ai/src/error/flags.ts:582-584` = hunk-1 anchor; `:586-594` = hunk-2 anchor).
- `flags.ts` is **unchanged** between 18.16.0 and 18.18.0: both files are 1154 lines and identical at every probed region (`:229-233`, `:582-595`, `:639-755`, `:757-803`, `:1100-1154` in both trees). No upstream commit touches this file in the 18.16.0→18.18.0 range (pi-ai `CHANGELOG.md:5-38` lists no error-classification change for `flags.ts`).

**2. Failing hunks** — none.

**3. Capability classification**

| cap | class | why / method | gate | effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0098 | **KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL** | Not ADOPT-CANDIDATE: 18.18.0 still has no pattern for this wording. `TRANSIENT_TRANSPORT_PATTERN` only contains `tls certificate verification failed` (`flags.ts:230`) and `isTransientErrorText` (`flags.ts:586-595`) has no TLS-verify arm; grep for `unknown certificate|certificate verification error` in the whole 18.18.0 nm tree hits only `pi-utils/src/tls-fetch.ts:9-10` (a doc comment about `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, present already in 18.16.0). Re-apply verbatim: keep the const + the extra `|| TRANSIENT_TLS_VERIFY_PATTERN.test(text)` in `isTransientErrorText`. Semantics intact — the classifier still routes persisted text through `isTransientErrorText` (`flags.ts:698-700`) via `classifyMessage` (`flags.ts:992-1008`), which is the path `TurnRecovery.#classifyRetryMessage` uses (`turn-recovery.ts:1457-1460`) and `isRetryableError` still ends in `AIError.retriable(id)` (`turn-recovery.ts:1562-1594`). Gate: `patches/apply.sh:588` → `patches/verify-tls-verify-transient-retry.ts` (5 assertions: 2 transient, 4 terminal controls); it only uses `TurnRecovery` + `Settings.isolated()`, both still present (`turn-recovery.ts:455`, `config/settings.ts:822`), so the gate runs unmodified. **S** (verbatim, unchanged file). Must be re-registered for the 18.18.0 arm in `apply.sh`/`verify-release-family-plan.py` when the new plan is generated (manifest + print-gates).

**4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes that could break the invariant despite clean hunks**
- *No cache-prefix / compaction / OAuth exposure*: this family only adds a regex to an error-classifier; it never touches prompt bytes, compaction, or credentials. Nothing in its owned path changed upstream.
- **Adjacent, low severity:** transcript `x-should-retry: false` is now a hard veto for the *transport* retry loop (`utils/openai-http.ts:115-119`, `providers/anthropic-client.ts:104-106`; changelog line "Provider retries no longer retry responses explicitly marked non-retryable", pi-ai `CHANGELOG.md:37`). Does not affect 0098: a TLS handshake failure yields no HTTP response/header, and 0098 acts on the *turn-recovery text* path, not on `shouldRetryResponse`.
- **Adjacent, no impact:** `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` wrapper (`pi-utils/src/tls-fetch.ts:1-35`, `pi-ai/src/utils/transport-fetch.ts:43-45`, `pi-catalog/src/utils.ts:9-11`, `config/model-registry.ts:640`) can make the error disappear when that env var is set, but it is not a classification; with the env var unset (our setup) nothing changes.
- Pre-existing (in 18.16.0 too, so not a regression): `networkFailureMessage` rewrites any `certificate verif…` text to `TLS certificate verification failed…` (`error/network-cause.ts:39-42,145-167`), which `TRANSIENT_TRANSPORT_PATTERN` matches, and `AnthropicConnectionError` is unconditional `Flag.Transient` (`flags.ts:789-790`). So *live* Anthropic fetch cert failures were already retried before 0098; 0098 is what covers persisted text from every provider. Gate's terminal controls are raw strings, so they still pass — no gate change needed.

**5. Worker-thread (`task.workerThreads`, default 4 — `task/settings.ts:438-440`): NO main-thread assumption.**
- The whole capability is a module-level `RegExp` constant + a pure string predicate in `pi-ai/src/error/flags.ts` (`:584` constant, `:597` call). No module singleton state, no timers, no extension runner, no session registry, no auth store. Each worker has its own VM/module graph (`thread/subagent-worker.ts:15-33`, "its own VM, module graph") which loads the *same patched file* from disk → identical behaviour.
- Classification happens inside the thread that owns the session: subagent sessions are created per placement (`task/executor.ts:4910-5012`) and each `AgentSession` constructs its own `TurnRecovery` (`session/agent-session.ts:2359`); the decision is `classifyMessage({errorMessage})` on persisted text, so nothing depends on sharing an object across threads and it survives any error/string serialization over the RPC link.
- Concrete failure mode if it were wrong (it is not, on current code): a subagent on a worker would treat `unknown certificate verification error` as terminal and its turn would end instead of retrying — a **visible task failure / wasted re-run**, not a silent cache or cost leak (the pattern never alters prompt bytes). Nothing in worker mode consumes cache prefix or OAuth here.
- Gate leg that would catch it: today the gate only exercises the in-process classifier, so a hypothetical worker-side divergence (e.g. a future worker boot path loading an unpatched module copy, or a worker-local guard bypassing `classifyText`) is uncaught. Add one leg to `verify-tls-verify-transient-retry.ts`: spawn a `Bun`/`node:worker_threads` Worker that imports `TurnRecovery` from the same roots and asserts the identical 5 cases (2 transient, 4 terminal), plus a negative control asserting `Settings.isolated()`-independent behaviour; cheap (<1 s), and PASS is required before shipping with `workerThreads=4`.

**Verdict:** keep 0098 as-is (verbatim rebase, S effort, gate unchanged), add the worker-thread gate leg, re-register the gate for the 18.18.0 arm.

## 0050-omp-session-history-preview — classification (18.16.0 → 18.18.0)

**Family**: `0050-omp-session-history-preview`, root NM, capability **0050** only.
Patch: `patches/.18.16.0-families/0050-omp-session-history-preview-v18.16.0.patch` — 5 files, 9 hunks
(`pi-coding-agent/{dist/types/,src/}session/session-listing`, `pi-tui/{dist/types/,src/}overlays/session-selector`, `pi-tui/src/overlays/session-info-overlay.ts`).

**Fuzz-0 on 18.18.0: CLEAN — 0 failing hunks.** Replay log `/home/chris/.cache/omp-plan-18180/replay-logs/0050-omp-session-history-preview.txt` lists all 5 files as "patching file" with no FAILED hunk. Manual verification of every touched site in the pristine 18.18.0 tree confirms each hunk still lands on the code it was written for (below).

### 2. Failing hunks
None. (Grouped cause analysis is therefore moot; the semantic checks below are what a re-audit would have covered.)

### 3. Per capability

**0050 — `omp -r` picker shows the session's last-used model ("last-model column"); `/session` info overlay offers Copy for Windows paths but not for `In-memory`.**
Class: **KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL**. Upstream still ships nothing equivalent:

- `SessionInfo` in 18.18.0 has no model field at all: `pi-coding-agent/src/session/session-listing.ts:35-59` (`status?: SessionStatus` at :58 is the last field). Producer site still shaped exactly as patched: `:495-507` (`firstMessage` :504, `allMessagesText` :505, `status: withStatus ? …` :506).
- `SessionSelectorEntry` likewise has no model field: `pi-tui/src/overlays/session-selector.ts:33-54`, and neither row renderer prints one — list row spans `:1208-1212`, wide/native metadata `:1319-1331`. The picker's own side preview shows Folder/Created/Modified/Size/Status/Forked-from only (`:168-201`), so the column is not redundant with upstream.
- `path.includes("/")` hunk: 18.18.0 still has the exact line `pi-tui/src/overlays/session-info-overlay.ts:233-236`; the `In-memory` sentinel it must not turn copyable is still produced the same way, `pi-coding-agent/src/modes/controllers/command-controller.ts:583` (`` `${stats.sessionFile ?? "In-memory"}` ``).
- Resume-side rule the column mirrors is unchanged: `EPHEMERAL_MODEL_CHANGE_ROLE = "fallback"` / `PREWALK_MODEL_CHANGE_ROLE = "prewalk"` (`pi-coding-agent/src/session/session-entries.ts:37,40`), role→model ordering `src/session/session-context.ts:133-150` (identical text to 18.16.0 `:133-145`), newest session-local (`temporary`/`prewalk`) restore `src/session/session-manager.ts:3902-3910`, ephemeral skip `:539-542`.

**Rebase method**: reapply the 18.16.0 patch verbatim (`patch -p1 --fuzz=0`), no hunk edits. Then only version lists in the gate need 18.18.0 (below).

**Owning gate**: `verify-session-history-preview.ts` (`patches/apply.sh:518`).
Gate edits required for 18.18.0 (mechanical, but mandatory — the gate throws before any assertion if missed):
- version allow-list `patches/verify-session-history-preview.ts:38` (hard `assert.ok` → loud failure, not silent),
- `:39` (`movedUi`), `:46` (`upstreamPickerPreview = agentVersion === "18.16.0"`, else the whole 18.16-style arm is skipped and the live 18.18.0 picker arm never runs), `:73` (`Reconciler` arm),
- fixture-version lists `:534` (`midFileSwitchPath`) and `:544` (`fallbackAfterGapPath`),
- `:681` worktree/reminder block and `:698` (`journaledReminders`) are other capabilities inside the same script — they must be consciously bumped by their owners (out of 0050 scope, but the gate cannot run on 18.18.0 until they are).

**Effort**: **S** (0 hunks to fix; ~8 literal version-list bumps in one verify script).

### 4. 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near owned paths that could silently break the invariant
All display-only; none touches prompt-cache bytes, compaction, OAuth or idle requests. The invariant is "the row shows the model resume will restore, in compact form".

- `SESSION_LIST_PREFIX_BYTES = 4096` / `SESSION_LIST_SUFFIX_BYTES = 32_768` unchanged (`session-listing.ts:73,79` vs 18.16.0 `:76,82`), and the tail is read only when `withStatus` (`:432-436`). Exported `listSessions` always passes `withStatus=true` (`:667-668`), so the picker path (`src/session/session-manager.ts:4722`) keeps its tail window. **Risk**: any future path that calls the status-less variant only gets 4 KB of prefix → `lastModel` degrades to the initial default (silent wrong label, no crash). Gate leg that covers it: `verify-session-history-preview.ts:613-674` (`lastModelOf` assertions built on `listSessions`, incl. `midFileSwitch` and `fallbackAfterGap`).
- Scan cache is an in-memory shallow copy under a stat key, not a serialization whitelist (`session-listing.ts:95-118`, hit path `:427-430`, store `:508-510`) → `lastModel` survives cache hits. **Risk if this ever became a persisted field-list**: column silently empty on warm lists; the gate's `modelScan` runs from a cold cache only, so a cache-roundtrip leg is a gap worth adding (cheap: second `listSessions()` call, assert `lastModel` still set).
- Role semantics: 18.18.0's `getRestorableSessionModels` skips `default`/`fallback` and otherwise uses `models[role]` (`session-context.ts:134-150`). Our `deriveLastModel` treats every non-`default`/non-`fallback` role as "that role's own model" and `temporary`/`prewalk` as the session-local restore (`session-manager.ts:3902`). Consistent today. **Residual, pre-existing**: a role that exists in history but not in `models` (role deleted from settings) makes the label show the transition's model while resume falls back to default (`session-context.ts:147-148`). Display-only; negative control = fixture with `role: "retired-role"`.
- 18.17.0 prewalk role is **not** new: `PREWALK_MODEL_CHANGE_ROLE = "prewalk"` already exists in 18.16.0 (`src/session/session-entries.ts:40` in the 18.16.0 tree), so no drift from the upgrade.

### 5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads` > 0)
**No — the capability does not assume main-only state, and worker mode cannot break it.**
Evidence: `deriveLastModel` is a pure function over two strings (patch lines 196-262 of the family patch: no module state, no timers, no session registry). The only process-global it touches is upstream's `fileSessionScanCache` / `kScanCache` LRU (`session-listing.ts:95-118`), which is a stat-keyed memo — a worker thread simply gets its own copy, and a cold miss only recomputes the same value. Both consumers are TUI/main-thread: the picker (`pi-tui/src/overlays/session-selector.ts`) and the `/session` info overlay (`session-info-overlay.ts:233`); the subagent worker does not import session-listing or any overlay (`pi-coding-agent/src/thread/subagent-worker.ts:26-39` — imports are `worker_threads`, `worker-host`, `task/settings`, `thread/*` only).
**Failure mode with `workerThreads>0`**: none — no crash, no cost, no cache effect; `lastModel` is a label that never reaches a provider request. Worst case if a hypothetical worker-homed listing were added later: a duplicated per-thread scan cache (extra file reads), never a wrong model.
**Gate leg that would catch a regression here**: none is needed for worker mode; if the plan wants coverage anyway, the existing `modelScan` arm (`verify-session-history-preview.ts:553-674`) already exercises the producer→row path in-process, and a worker-mode smoke would only need to assert the picker still renders the column after a `workerThreads=4` subagent run.

### Summary
0050 is the simplest family in the batch: **KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL, 9/9 hunks apply at fuzz-0, effort S**. No ADOPT-CANDIDATE (upstream never adopted a last-model field), no retirement, no conflict. Work at upgrade time = reapply verbatim + add `"18.18.0"` to 8 version literals in `patches/verify-session-history-preview.ts` (:38,:39,:46,:73,:534,:544 and the non-0050 :681/:698 by their owners). Consider adding one cheap cache-roundtrip assertion to close the only genuine silent-failure gap.

- No upstream commit subject needed: the region was not touched in v18.16.0..v18.18.0 (no changelog entry under prune/supersede in `pi-coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md` 18.17/18.18 sections; `pi-agent-core/CHANGELOG.md` last pruning change is 18.8.x-era). Cause of clean apply = **no upstream change to the logic or the context**.

### 3. Per capability
| cap | class | rebase method | gate | effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0046 | **KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL** | Re-anchor the single hunk by symbol, not by line: the config object passed to `pruneSupersededToolResults(...)` at `session-maintenance.ts:807` must end with `suffixTokenLimit: 0,` replacing `suffixTokenLimit: model?.thinking?.prefixBinding === true ? 0 : undefined,` (`session-maintenance.ts:820`). Keep the rationale comment (it prices the 8 000 gate against the unobservable provider checkpoint stride). Do **not** touch the sibling `pruneToolOutputs` call at `:770` (`cacheWarmSuffixTokens`) — different knob, upstream-owned. | `verify-supersede-warm-prefix-guard.ts`, registered unconditionally at `patches/apply.sh:514` | S (apply + rerun gate; no source surgery) |

Semantics still hold on 18.18.0, verified against the actual loop rather than by analogy:
- Non-idle branch: `pruning.ts:510-524` starts `suffixTokens = 0` and tests `suffixTokens <= suffixTokenLimit` *before* accumulating, so with limit 0 only a candidate sitting on the very last branch entry is rewritten; every other candidate defers. With `suffixTokenLimit: 0` the `while (next >= 0 && candidates[next].index === i)` line can therefore only fire at `i === entries.length - 1`.
- A supersede candidate always has its successor after it, so that case is unreachable for `supersedeReads`; the `dropUseless` half of the same pass (`pruneUseless: dropUseless`, `session-maintenance.ts:814`) keeps the immediate path — exactly what the patch comment claims. Both branches still present in 18.18.0.
- Idle flush still reclaims: `pruning.ts:495-497` (`idle` from `idleFlushMs`, default 90 min at `session-maintenance.ts:321`).

### 4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near the owned paths that could silently break the invariant
1. **`task.workerThreads` (18.17.0, default 4)** — `thread/subagent-worker.ts:24` ("`task.workerThreads` is read live on every placement", default at `task/settings.ts:438-442`). Subagent sessions now run in workers; each constructs its **own** `SessionMaintenance` (`session/agent-session.ts:2988`) and therefore its own supersede pass with its own tokenizer/model/session manager. Invariant is per-session, so it carries. Risk is *coverage*, not correctness: our gate only exercises the main-thread fixture, so a worker-homed session silently prunes warm prefixes only if the patch failed to land there — which cannot happen (same file, same process image). Severity: low.
2. **New prompt-cache observability** — `display.cacheMissMarker` / `cache` status segment (`CHANGELOG.md:22`, `:65`; implementation `session/agent-session.ts:6139 getPromptCacheStatus`, `modes/controllers/command-controller.ts:667-673`). No behavior change, but see gate leg (b) below: this is the cheapest runtime detector for "no crash, silent cache loss".
3. **Idle compaction now suppressed on never-expiring caches** (`CHANGELOG.md:773`) and idle compaction timing around the 1 h TTL (`CHANGELOG.md:951`). With the guard deferring all supersede rewrites to the 90-minute flush, an idle compaction may now win the race and summarize the deferred entry away. Outcome is still "no warm-prefix rewrite", so the invariant holds; the deferred token reclaim is done by compaction instead. Severity: low, worth one line in the plan so nobody reads the 90-min flush as guaranteed.
4. **Adjacent, unchanged coverage gap (not a regression)**: the sibling pass still rewrites at 8 000 suffix tokens for non-`prefixBinding` models (`session-maintenance.ts:770`). 0046 only closes the supersede pass. Do not widen scope; record it as the reason the new `cache` segment may still occasionally show a mid-history rewrite from `#pruneToolOutputs`.
5. Nothing in the range touches `SUPERSEDED_NOTICE`, `readToolSupersedeKey`, `blankToolResult`, or `estimatePrunedSavings` — the wire signature the cost model is priced against is stable (`pruning.ts:118`).

### 5. Worker-thread assumption
**No** — the capability does **not** depend on main-thread/process-global state, on any thread it is not homed to.
- Evidence: it is a single literal argument (`suffixTokenLimit: 0`) plus locals read from the owning session: `this.#host.sessionManager.getBranchView()`, `this.#host.model()`, `this.#tokenizer` (`session-maintenance.ts:804-809`). `model` is now used **only** for `cacheLookbackPositions` (`:821`), so the patch actually removes the one per-session-model branch — the guard is model-independent.
- Worker subagents are real sessions: `thread/worker-warm-up.ts:47` builds one through `createAgentSession`, workers publish/host it (`thread/services/agents.ts:107-111`), and `SessionMaintenance` is constructed per `AgentSession` (`session/agent-session.ts:2988`). Therefore the pass executes inside the worker against the worker's own session — identical code, identical result. No module singleton, no cross-thread map, no timer ownership (the idle clock is just `now - timestamp` inside `pruning.ts:495`).
- Concrete failure mode with `workerThreads > 0`: none attributable to 0046 (no crash, no extra spend, no cache break). The only conceivable silent-cost path would be the guard silently not being applied in a worker, which is impossible because the constant is in the same file the worker links; it would be caught by gate leg (c) if someone later conditionalized it on thread identity.

### 6. Gates this plan must carry for 0046
Keep `verify-supersede-warm-prefix-guard.ts` (apply.sh:514) as-is; it already has the negative control (stock limit `undefined` must prune 1 → proves the fixture reproduces the bug) and the deferral/leak legs. Two additions worth landing during implementation:
- (a) **Source leg re-anchor**: the existing `source.indexOf("pruneSupersededToolResults(")` remains valid on 18.18.0 (the import line at `session-maintenance.ts:52` has no `(`, so the slice starts at the real call at `:807`), but the end anchor `indexOf("}),")` now resolves at `:851`. Keep it, and assert the slice also contains `idleFlushMs: PRUNE_IDLE_FLUSH_MS` so a future upstream insert cannot make the slice vacuous.
- (b) **Silent-cost leg** (the class the current suite cannot see): after a mid-history supersede candidate is deferred, assert `session.getPromptCacheStatus()` (`agent-session.ts:6139`) still reports the prefix as warm / no re-send, i.e. deferral does not itself cost a re-read. Positive control: run the same fixture with the stock limit and require the status to flip. This is the leg that would have caught the original 40 832→32 640 regression at the harness level rather than via payload forensics.
- (c) **Worker leg**: with `task.workerThreads > 0`, assert a subagent-homed session's `#pruneStaleToolResults` also defers (cheapest form: run the existing fixture against a `SessionMaintenance` built with a worker-homed host, or assert the literal in the worker's loaded module like the source leg). Marked optional — 0046 has no thread-sensitive state, so this is regression insurance, not a correctness requirement.

**Net for the plan**: 0046 needs no redesign; one-line mechanical re-anchor, unchanged gate, effort S. No retirement, no semantic rewrite.

# Family `0095-omp-context-audit-request-purpose` (root AGENT, capability 0095)

## 1. Fuzz-0 status on 18.18.0
- Applies **cleanly at `--fuzz=0`**: `replay-logs/0095-omp-context-audit-request-purpose.txt` reports **0 FAILED hunks** for all 9 files (offsets only: runner.ts +129, sdk.ts +43, agent-session.ts +110/+147, runner.d.ts +11, agent-session-types.* +1).

All three `dist/types/*.d.ts` still exist upstream (`18.18.0/nm/@oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent/dist/types/extensibility/extensions/runner.d.ts` present), so the published-type half of the patch still has a target.
- Independent of the main's corrected counts (my family was already in the applying set: 0 failed hunks).

## 2. Per-hunk cause
Every hunk: **context drift** (line offsets only). No hunks; upstream did not rewrite these sites:
- `src/extensibility/extensions/runner.ts:2363` `emitContext(messages, signal)` — still no `purpose`; body at `:2372-2422` is the same shape as 18.16.0 (`18.16.0/.../runner.ts:2234-2296`), including the identical `setContextHistoryIndex`/`markPerCallContextMessage` bookkeeping. Event still built at `:2388` as `{ type: "context", messages }`.
- `src/extensibility/shared-events.ts:192-196` — `ContextEvent` still has only `type` + `messages`; no upstream `purpose`/`side-request` anywhere in `src/` (grep: no `side-request`, no `purpose` field on any event).
- `src/extensibility/hooks/runner.ts:373-383` — unchanged; this `emitContext` has **no callers** in 18.18.0 (grep: only the definition), so that hunk is compile-only.
- `src/sdk.ts:4650-4653` `transformContext` (2-arg) and its two consumptions `:4855` (Agent option) / `:5167` (AgentSession config) — unchanged.
- `src/session/agent-session-types.ts:324` (`transformContext?` 2-arg), `src/session/agent-session.ts:1294` (`#transformContext` field), `:2431` (assignment), `:2676` (`SessionProviderBoundaryHost.transformContext` closure) — unchanged.

## 3. Capability classification
**Class: KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL** (not ADOPT-CANDIDATE).

Method: re-apply the patch unchanged at fuzz-0; no code edits needed. Only change is bookkeeping in the gate: `patches/verify-context-persist-audit.ts:24` `SUPPORTED_PLANS` lacks `"18.18.0"` and `:30-34` asserts `agentVersion ∈ SUPPORTED_PLANS`, so the gate fails closed on the new tree until `"18.18.0"` is added (the 18.16.0 arm's behaviour is what we want: fresh isolated HOME, real SDK, mock provider).

Why it is still load-bearing (negative proof that upstream did NOT adopt it): the incident category survives 18.18.0. Compaction-prefix conversion still runs through the *shared* pipeline — `agent-session.ts:2924-2941` (`buildLiveProviderContext` → `convertMessagesToLlm`), `:7771-7774` (`convertMessagesToLlm` → `#providerBoundary.convertMessagesToLlm`), `session-provider-boundary.ts:212-215` (→ `#host.transformContext`). Only the marker (purpose) distinguishes it from a real turn; nothing upstream does.

- Owning gate: `patches/apply.sh:555` `_omp_gate 0095 verify-context-persist-audit.ts`.
- Gate legs that matter after rebase: `verify-context-persist-audit.ts:359-374` — real SDK + mock provider, (a) `session.convertMessagesToLlm(messages.slice(0,1))` must add **no** audit line, (b) live history not truncated, (c) `agent.replaceMessages(truncated)` + `agent.continue()` must still produce `status: file-extra`. This pair is the negative control that keeps the label honest (a `turn`-only audit that sees nothing would fail (c)).
- Effort: **S** (add version to the plan list + rerun).

## 4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near the owned paths that could break the invariant
| Change | Evidence | Severity for 0095 | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Subagents move to worker threads (`task.workerThreads`, default 4) | `src/task/settings.ts:438-440`; `src/thread/subagent-worker.ts`; CHANGELOG `18.17.0` "Many concurrent subagents now run in worker threads" | **Medium** — new threads execute `context` handlers; the audit consumer's log is a shared file | needs new worker leg (see §5) |
| Worker-thread subagents no longer throw `Cannot send … instance across threads` when the model comes from an extension-registered provider with its own `streamSimple` | CHANGELOG `18.18.0` fix, PR #18310 | **High (positive)** — our gate fixture is exactly that shape (`context-persist-audit-extension.ts` + `pi.registerProvider(... streamSimple ...)`, gate `:344-356`); on 18.17.0 it would throw once `workerThreads>0` | same worker leg |
| Prepared extensions are re-imported per thread (module-level state becomes per-thread) | `src/thread/services/integrations.ts:16-20, 218-245`; `sdk.ts:3747` builds one `ExtensionRunner` per `createAgentSession` | Low — no shared mutable state in *our* patch; consumer duplicates its own maps per thread | none |
| Main-turn path unchanged: `Agent` calls `transformContext(messages, signal)` only | `pi-agent-core/src/agent-loop.ts:1970-1974, 1995-1997`; `sdk.ts:4650-4653` is that closure | Low (good) — third param defaults to `"turn"`, so main turns stay audited | (c) above |
| Session keeps its own setting value when `config.yml` gains an unknown key, preserving tools + prompt cache | CHANGELOG `18.18.0` line 66 | Low/none for 0095; relevant to settings-gated families | — |
| Skill routing hints re-compressed in the system prompt | CHANGELOG `18.18.0` "Skills listed in the system prompt get their compressed routing hint…" | None for 0095; one-time prefix byte change on upgrade | belongs to persistent-prefix-resume family |

No cache-prefix, OAuth or compaction-chain invariant of this capability is touched: the patch only adds a parameter, never reorders messages.

## 5. Worker-thread assumption
**Answer: NO for the patched code; YES-adjacent for its consumer (silent, not crash).**

- Patch code is per-`AgentSession` state only: `agent-session.ts:1294` field, `:2676` closure over `#transformContext`, `sdk.ts:4650` closure over that session's `extensionRunner` (`sdk.ts:3747` creates one runner per `createAgentSession`, i.e. per thread). No module singleton, timer, global registry or auth store is read or written. Worker-hosted child sessions get their own session object (`task/executor.ts:5033` `createAgentSession`) and therefore their own `"turn"`/`"side-request"` labelling — behaviour is identical on main and in a worker.
- The concrete risk with `workerThreads>0` is on the **consumer** side: the audit extension keeps `const lastSignature = new Map<string,string>()` at extension-factory scope (`patches/context-persist-audit-extension.ts:290`, used at `:316`) and appends to one process-wide file `~/.omp/agent/context-persist-audit.log` (`:71`, `:286`) with 1 MiB rotation via `statSync`+`renameSync` (`:285`). Child sessions inherit the prepared extension (`task/executor.ts:4961`, `task/structured-subagent.ts:858`), so subagent `context` events now fire from several threads into the same file. Failure mode: **silent** — interleaved or lost JSONL records (rotation race between threads), never a crash and never extra cost/cache loss; the per-thread `lastSignature` map stays correct (it is per thread, keyed by `sessionId`).
- Gate leg that would catch it: today **none** — `verify-context-persist-audit.ts:304-374` drives only the main thread with a single session. Required new leg: run the same block with `"task.workerThreads": 4` in the `Settings.loadIsolated` overrides (`:330-337`), spawn one `task` subagent through the real executor, then assert (i) no audit record for any `side-request` conversion, (ii) the truncated-main-turn record still appears with the main `sessionId`, (iii) every line in the log parses as JSON and no record is lost across a forced rotation. That leg is the "PASS" criterion for turning `task.workerThreads` on at 4.

Effort for the whole item (rebase unchanged + gate version arm + worker leg): **S/M** (worker leg is the only new code; S if the subagent spawn is driven the way family-0101's gate already spawns one).

## 0048-omp-advisor-quota-rotation-bound — classification on 18.18.0

**1. Family / capability / replay**
- Family `0048-omp-advisor-quota-rotation-bound`, root `NM` (plan.tsv:9), capability `0048` only (manifest.json families[0048].capabilities = ["0048"]). Owned paths: `pi-coding-agent/src/session/session-advisors.ts`, `pi-tui/src/overlays/advisor-config.ts` (manifest.json paths).

Replay result `/home/chris/.cache/omp-plan-18180/replay-18.18.0/pi-coding-agent/src/session/session-advisors.ts:2170-2187` holds the patched rotation branch, so the apply is real, not a skipped file.

**2. Failing hunks** — none. Re-anchor drift is small and benign: the `@@ -2149,7 +2161,26 @@` usage-limit branch now sits at 18.18.0 `session-advisors.ts:2152` (`if (outcome.switched && this.#spendCredentialSwitch(...)) return true;`), i.e. ~+3 lines of unrelated drift above it; hunk 2's `retryFallbackPendingSuccess`/`retryFallback?` neighbours are at 18.18.0:351-352; hunk 1's anchor `const ADVISOR_CODEX_SSE_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 1;` is unchanged at 18.18.0:140 (patched form: replay file:147 `ADVISOR_USAGE_LIMIT_ROTATION_GRACE = 1`). No upstream commit subject is reachable in this read-only session (no shell/git access); the byte-level comparison below is the evidence instead.

**3. Capability 0048 — class: KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL (effort S), with one S-sized semantic hardening**
- Invariant: one same-provider credential rotation per advisor per quota episode, then the cross-provider fallback chain; reset on a landed turn. Rebase method: keep the patch verbatim (`patch -p1 --fuzz=0` reproduces it), bump nothing but the gate's version pin; no upstream API it touches changed (see §4).
- Owning gate: `verify-advisor-quota-rotation.ts`, selected by `_omp_gate 0048 …` at `patches/apply.sh:614`. **It hard-pins the tree version**: `assert.equal(version, "18.16.0", "this gate pins the 18.16.0 tree; another version fails closed")` at `patches/verify-advisor-quota-rotation.ts:89` → the implement phase MUST raise that to `18.18.0`, else the gate fails closed on the new tree (that is the only rebase edit the gate needs; its four legs drive the real `SessionAdvisors` plus the real fallback-chain resolver).
- **Defect found in the current patch (recommend fixing during the rebase, S):** the grace is cleared only on turn success (hunk 3, replay file:1639) and on `switched === false` (replay file:2186). It is *not* cleared by `#resetRecoveryBudgets`, which upstream calls on turn success (18.18.0:1626), turn abandonment (1637-1640), conversation start (1087) and post-compaction rebuild (1919-1920), and which resets `providerWaitRetries` + `credentialSwitches` there (`session-advisors.ts:2277-2280`). Consequence: after an abandoned advisor turn the pool budget is refreshed but the already-spent grace is not, so the next quota episode on the same provider gets zero rotations (premature cross-provider hop or latch) — a behaviour asymmetry, not a crash. Fix: add `advisor.usageLimitRotations = undefined;` inside `#resetRecoveryBudgets` (18.18.0:2277-2280) and drop the duplicated success-path line if desired; gate legs 2/4 still pass, and a new leg should assert "abandon → next episode gets its one rotation again".
- Gate coverage gap (pre-existing, must stay documented): the two `pi-tui/src/overlays/advisor-config.ts` hunks are explicitly out of the gate's scope (`verify-advisor-quota-rotation.ts:27-32`), because the terminal render draws from `SelectList` while only the native sheet sees `TspPickerProps`, and `#nativeEditor` (`advisor-config.ts:358`) is private. If the plan wants them covered, the only public handle is `handleNativeEvent` (`advisor-config.ts:802`, `NativeUiEvent` → `#route`) which needs a deps fixture — effort M, optional. Otherwise carry the existing written justification into the 18.18.0 plan.

**4. Upstream changes 18.16.0 → 18.18.0 near the owned paths that could silently break the invariant**
- `pi-ai/src/auth/rotation.ts` `#blockCredentialForRotation` + `markReached` are **textually identical** in both trees (18.18.0:120-225 vs 18.16.0:120-225); `switched: true` is still returned whenever *any* same-type sibling has no live block (`rotation.ts:200-213`). So the zero-backoff ping-pong this capability exists to stop is unchanged upstream — no adoption candidate, and the "upstream already does it" class does not apply.
- Advisor runtime's zero-backoff rerun is also unchanged: `advisor/runtime.ts` matches line-for-line at every recovery anchor (`onTurnError` call 1528-1536, `recovered` branch 1564-1571, quota latch 1583-1601) in both trees. If only one of the two had changed, the grace would become moot or double-count; it did not.
- The pool-traversal budget `#spendCredentialSwitch` (auth-derived `credentials.list(provider).length - 1`, `session-advisors.ts:2269-2274`) already exists in 18.16.0 at the identical line, so the patch's two-tier bound (grace=1 before N-1) still layers correctly.
- No new pre-turn credential preflight can bypass the patched branch: `session/session-provider-boundary.ts` in 18.18.0 contains no `rotate`/`markReached`/credential-limit logic (grep: zero matches).
- pi-tui hunks still mean what they meant: `selectListPicker` still spreads `o.decorate?.(item)` over the label (`pi-tui/src/native/picker.ts:212-220`) and pristine 18.18.0 still filters the sentinel out of the native payload (`advisor-config.ts:713-715`, `TOOLS_DONE` at 231) while the tools screen still decorates `item => ({ label: item.value })` at 1647. Both hunks therefore still (a) keep the `__done` row in the native sheet so `handleNativeEvent`'s `confirm`/`activate` path can apply the selection, and (b) label it `Done` instead of the raw sentinel (`picker.ts:255-268` resolves by `item.value`, so the row's id must stay `__done`).
- Cache-prefix / compaction / OAuth: nothing. This family touches no prompt bytes, no compaction chain, no credential refresh — its only auth interaction is read (`credentials.list`) plus the forwarded `markReached` verdict.

**5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads`, default 4; `thread/subagent-worker.ts`) — verdict: no, not process-global-dependent; no crash, no extra spend. One watch item.**
- Advisors can run inside a worker thread: subagents default to unadvised (`task/executor.ts:1252-1254`, `"advisor.enabled": false`) but opt in per agent (`executor.ts:4470-4476`) and via persisted revive (`task/persisted-revive.ts:363-381`); advisor state is mirrored to main (`thread/session-mirror.ts:357-358`, `thread/session-publisher.ts:509-511`), which only exists because the session can be homed in a worker.
- The capability's own counter (`ActiveAdvisor.usageLimitRotations`, replay file:364-365) lives on the `SessionAdvisors`/`ActiveAdvisor` instance created per `AgentSession` (`session/agent-session.ts:2840`). That is per-thread by construction and needs no sharing — an advisor in a worker keeps its own grace, exactly as each advisor already keeps its own `credentialSwitches`.
- The *shared* verdict comes from main: in a worker, `authStorage.limits.markReached`/`rotate` are async RPC forwards (`thread/services/auth-remote.ts:557-570`) and `credentials.list` is a **sync** forward to main (`auth-remote.ts:249-251`), with blocks upserted to main (`auth-remote.ts:613-623`). So `switched` and the pool size N stay single-sourced, and the ping-pong dynamics are identical with workerThreads>0.
- Concrete failure mode with workerThreads>0: none that increases spend. The only thread-sensitive edge is the synchronous `credentials.list` call inside `#spendCredentialSwitch` (`thread/rpc.ts:459`, `DEFAULT_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS = 120_000` at `rpc.ts:169`): if main (TUI) is wedged >120 s the call throws instead of hanging, the throw escapes `#recoverAdvisorTurn`, and `AdvisorRuntime` catches it as a failed `onTurnError` hook (`advisor/runtime.ts:1534-1536`) → `recovered = false` → the advisor declines/latches quota with **less** traffic, not more. Worst case across threads is bounded: with `workerThreads: 4`, at most one rotation per advisor instance (main + up to 4 advised subagents), never an unbounded ping-pong.
- Gate leg that would catch a wrong count: legs 2 and 3 already assert exact recovery counts and the provider sequence (`verify-advisor-quota-rotation.ts:379-411`) and leg 4 the per-provider re-grant (413-426); they are process-agnostic because the auth layer under test is the same forwarding interface. Gaps to close in the implement phase (cheap→M): (a) the abandon-path leg described in §3, (b) optionally one leg that builds the same fixture inside a worker thread (or one that stubs `credentials.list` to throw, pinning "sync-RPC failure ⇒ decline, not spin"), so a worker-mode regression shows red rather than silently passing.

- Gate only asserts the pure function (verify-retry-slow-schedule.ts:15 deepEqual of 9 attempts with baseDelayMs=500); it proves the table, not that a real retry path consumes it.
- Intent: PATCHES.md:65 (fixed schedule for turn recovery + advisors; provider retry-after and retry.maxDelayMs still apply; retry.baseDelayMs ignored; user config retry.maxRetries:8).

### 4. Upstream 18.16.0->18.18.0 changes near owned paths (silent-break risk)
No `git log` available to me (read-only tools, no shell); established by direct tree comparison:
- Retry setting surface identical: session/settings.ts retry block same IDs, defaults and line numbers in both trees — retry.maxRetries 10 (724-726), retry.baseDelayMs 500 (742), retry.maxDelayMs 5min (745-747), retry.rateLimitWalksChain 811, usageReserve* 837/858, fallbackChains 889, fallbackRevertPolicy 902. No new retry knob added.
- turn-recovery.ts:2690-2800 pacing block unchanged in shape: bounded-thinking maxRetries clamp, `calculateRetryBackoffDelayMs(retrySettings.baseDelayMs, this.#retryAttempt)` at 2717, rate-limit reason override takes max at 2736-2737, credential-read pacing `Math.max(1_000, result.retryAfterMs, calculateRetryBackoffDelayMs(...))` at 2778-2779, maxDelayMs budget break at 2781.
- Invariants hold: provider retry-after stays authoritative (2713-2716, 2729-2738) so no provider window is shortened; maxDelayMs 300s vs 8-attempt ladder 66s (no silent truncation of retry.maxRetries:8); rate-limit backoff still overrides upward (`calculateRateLimitBackoffMs` also present in 18.16.0 at turn-recovery.ts:20,836,1897); Anthropic slow-lane (pi-ai/src/providers/anthropic-slow-mode.ts, pi-ai/src/types.ts:768, anthropic.ts:2519-2526,2724-2727) already exists in 18.16.0 and is independent of this function.
- Cache prefix / compaction / OAuth: no interaction (returns a number, holds no state).
- Cosmetic only: upstream comment turn-recovery.ts:2718-2722 still says "default exponential base (~500ms, capped at 8s)" — stale prose, not a behavior issue; do not patch it (needless rebase surface).

### 5. Worker-thread assumption (task.workerThreads default 4, task/settings.ts:438-442)
**No** — no process-global/main-thread reliance.
- Patched code is an immutable module-level const read by a pure function (patch:8-21); module state is per-thread in Bun workers, values identical.
- Retry settings reach the worker via the Settings mirror (thread/services/settings.ts:48-67,175-215,285-300, applyMirrorState 292; auth/mirror at thread/services/auth.ts:353-396), so maxRetries/maxDelayMs/baseDelayMs resolve identically in a worker.
- Concrete failure mode with workerThreads>0: none expected — the retry sleeps run inside the worker's own turn-recovery; a busy worker is not reaped (idle timer not armed while `worker.load > 0`, subagent-worker.ts:519-530) and the only hard deadline is the 30s **init** timeout (subagent-worker.ts:41,237-244,257-262), inactive during a turn, so a 15s retry cannot trip a watchdog.
- Behaviour to price, not fix: a retrying subagent holds its worker slot for up to ~66s of sleeps; unproven today that the schedule actually runs in worker mode.
- Gate legs to add so a worker-mode regression is catchable:
  1. mirror leg — spawn a worker_threads worker, bind the real settings mirror, assert cfgRetryMaxRetries.get(settings)===8 and cfgRetryMaxDelayMs.get(settings)===300000 inside it.
  2. schedule-in-worker leg — import retry-fallback-chains.ts in that worker and deep-equal the 9-element table against the main-thread result (catches worker retries silently reverting to 500ms exponential — no existing gate covers this).
  3. optional integration leg — task.workerThreads=1, subagent against a synthetic 500-twice provider, assert consecutive request timestamps >=2.9s apart.

### Conclusion
0099 = clean one-hunk mechanical keep: 0 failing hunks, identical pre-image at retry-fallback-chains.ts:89-97, same three call sites/lines, unchanged retry settings block, worker-thread-safe by construction. Work item: reapply, extend verify-retry-slow-schedule.ts with the §5 mirror + schedule-in-worker legs, re-run apply.sh:589. Effort S.

### 3. Capability classification
**0074 → KEEP-REBASE-SEMANTIC** (applies mechanically, must be corrected after apply).
- Rebase method: (a) keep `isPerCallContextMessage` in the import list of `anthropic.ts` — drop only the other part of hunk 1's edit, since 18.18.0 has a live consumer; (b) add `"18.18.0"` to `SUPPORTED_PLANS` in both gates and add an explicit thread arm (below); everything else (barrier removal, cacheable-block predicate, tail-candidate eligibility, sub-prefix anchor removal, image stable cap, audit wiring, rebuildParams) is unchanged by upstream and re-applies as-is.
- Owning gates: `verify-anthropic-cache-boundaries.ts`, `verify-anthropic-request-audit.ts` (registered `apply.sh:536-537` under `_omp_plan_has 0074`).
- Effort: **S** (2-line import fix + arm additions), gate arms **M** if the thread/concurrency legs are added.

### 4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near owned paths that can break the invariant
1. **Anthropic message threads, default ON for official API** (`pi-ai/CHANGELOG.md:11`; `anthropic-threads.ts`; rule `supportsMessageThreads` at `pi-catalog/src/compat/axes.ts:278`, `resolve.ts:906`, `rules.json` class `anthropic`, providers `anthropic`, revision ≥4.5). A `continue` sends only appended messages and re-derives breakpoints (`anthropic-threads.ts:235-247`, `continueBreakpoints:419-456`), so the provider-visible prefix is no longer "the full body + our 4 breakpoints". **Severity: HIGH** for cache-prefix reasoning; owner gate `verify-anthropic-cache-boundaries.ts` (needs a thread arm). Mitigating facts found: threading is skipped when any message carries the per-call marker (`anthropic.ts:2261`), when there is no `providerSessionState` (`:2259`), and for compaction/credit/`clear_at` bodies (`anthropic-threads.ts:327-331`); our Trellis context injection marks every request per-call (`PATCHES.md:1672`), so deployed sessions stay unthreaded — but that is a load-bearing coincidence, not tested.
2. **Gates abort on the new version** (both files, see §2) — **HIGH**, blocks the apply gate.
3. Cache warmer unchanged (`pi-coding-agent/src/session/cache-warmer.ts:762` still `maxTokens: 1`), so the audit's `refresh` heuristic (`anthropic-request-audit.ts` `refresh: options?.maxTokens === 1`) stays valid — severity none.
4. Compaction wire shape unchanged: `compaction: {type:"summarize"}` (`anthropic.ts:5488-5493`), legacy edit `compact_20260112` (`:1469`, `:1476`), replay block `type:"compaction"` (`:1410-1418`) — the audit's `native-compaction`/`compaction-replay` kinds stay valid; severity none.
5. `anthropic-images.ts` unchanged upstream and the 18.18 "thin tool images are padded" fix is not in pi-ai (no match for padding/minimum-height in `pi-ai/src`) ⇒ image-prefix invariant unaffected; severity none.
6. Cache-facing **session** changes: unknown `config.yml` keys no longer reset settings and drop tools (`pi-coding-agent/CHANGELOG.md:66`), cache-miss marker now detects tail re-sends (`:65`), `cache` status segment added (`:22`). Informational only; no write-path change. One-time cold cache after upgrade due to system-prompt/skill-hint changes (`CHANGELOG.md:76,178`) — expected, no gate.
7. `textVerbosity` default low (`CHANGELOG.md:121`) affects Codex only, not this family.

### 5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads`, default 4)
**Answer: no hard main-thread-only dependency, but two concrete cross-thread hazards.**
Evidence for "no": the whole capability is per-request state — `createAnthropicRequestAudit` state is a closure local (`anthropic-request-audit.ts`, `let current`), `applyPromptCaching` is pure over `params`, and the refs end up in `preparedContext`/`params` only (`anthropic.ts:2268-2330`). Subagents placed in worker threads run a full `AgentSession` inside that worker (`task/executor.ts:4383-4386`, `thread/subagent-worker.ts:19-60`), which builds its own `#providerSessionState` (`session/agent-session.ts:1442,2800`) — state is per-isolate, nothing is shared.
Hazards:
- **H1 (silent, diagnostics only):** audit identity is `sha256(sessionId ?? promptCacheKey, provider, endpoint, model)`; same-type sibling subagents may share one `promptCacheKey` (`agent-session.ts:13271`, `getPromptCacheKeyForModel`). Two workers with no `sessionId` would `renameSync` onto the same `<identity>.baseline.json` and then compare full-vs-appended thread bodies, producing false `prefix-different` rows. Failure mode: **no crash, no cost** — corrupted diagnostics, so the audit can no longer prove the cache chain. Not caught by any leg: `verify-anthropic-request-audit.ts:98-109` is single-threaded and always passes `sessionId: "audit-session"`.
- **H2 (CPU only):** `anthropicImageResizeCaches` is a module-level `Map` (`anthropic-images.ts:81`), so each worker re-encodes the same screenshots. Output is deterministic (`Bun.Image … png()/jpeg()` with fixed quality, `anthropic-images.ts:141-143`, no timestamps/randomness), so the prefix still matches — **silent extra CPU, not extra tokens**. Uncatchable today because both gates run in one realm.
- Also relevant to H2/H1: the hunk-1 import break crashes on every thread equally, so a worker-thread gate would have caught it; the current gates cannot.
Gate legs that close this (implementation-phase work):
1. Worker leg in `verify-anthropic-cache-boundaries.ts`: run the same fixture on the main thread and inside ≥2 `Bun.Worker`s with `task.workerThreads` semantics, with per-call markers **off** and **on**, and assert byte-identical captures (message breakpoint indexes, image dims, system head) — catches "cache not connected" that a crash-only check misses.
2. Concurrency leg in `verify-anthropic-request-audit.ts`: two concurrent `streamAnthropic` calls with the same `promptCacheKey` and no `sessionId`, asserting two distinct baselines (or a `threadId/pid`-qualified identity) and that neither call reports `prefix-different`.
3. Make both gates PASS with `task.workerThreads=4`; if either leg cannot be made to pass, ship with `0` plus the reason (per DECISIONS).

Notes: upstream commit subjects were not obtainable from this worker (no shell tool; `upstream.git` is a bare pack). Provenance is asserted from the shipped 18.18.0 `CHANGELOG.md`/`pi-ai/CHANGELOG.md` entries and code anchors above; PR/commit mapping is `[INFERENCE]` from those entries.

- **MEDIUM — `followMCPTools(..., { scope: reopened.getSessionId() }, ...)` is new** (`executor.ts:4293-4295`); re-anchoring must not drop the scope argument, else MCP proxy tools bind to the wrong scope and the revived tool set (and thus the tool-schema half of the prefix) differs — gate `a1`/`b` tool-schema assertions (`verify-…ts:557-563`).

- No change to `session_init` schema (`session-entries.ts:319-321`), `extractSessionInit` (`session-manager.ts:4798-4826`) or `open()` options used here.

### 5. Worker-thread assumption → **NO** (patch is worker-safe as written)
Evidence: the reviver body runs on the thread that owns the session — for a worker-homed kept-alive agent that is the worker (adoption crosses as a remote callable, `rpc.ts:19,697-701`; main's ledger calls it back, `thread/services/agents.ts:166-190`; resident sessions keep a worker alive, `thread/subagent-worker.ts:16-24`). Everything the patch adds is thread-local: `extractSessionInit(reopened.getEntries())` on a locally opened manager, a closure over a local array, `revived.setWorkPoolYieldItems(...)` on the local session. The only globals it touches are reached through worker proxies: `AgentRegistry.installGlobal` installs main's registry as a `RemoteAgentRegistry` (`agents.ts:404-409`, `agents-remote.ts:99-160`, all `callSync`), so `refreshSubagentIrcRoot`/`syncSessionStatus` still reach main.
- Contrast (why this question is live): upstream's *pre-18.18* `heldIrcRoster` read `AgentRegistry.global().get(id)?.session?.baseSystemPrompt` (18.16.0:4021-4024) — in a worker that is a `RemoteAgentSession` with no `baseSystemPrompt`, so the held roster silently vanished. 18.18.0 replaced it with `ownAttachedSession` + `isLocalAgentSession` (`executor.ts:3801-3804`, `:4149`). Our override bypasses `baseSystemPrompt` entirely, so it is immune to that failure mode.
- Concrete failure mode with `workerThreads>0` if the rebase were done sloppily (e.g. reading items/roster from a main-only cache): **no crash, silent cost/cache loss** — the revived session renders `workPoolYieldItems: []`, appends a `session_init` at the first model call, and the provider sees a changed tool schema + system block → full prefix rewrite.
- **Gate gap (actionable)**: `verify-subagent-warm-revival-prompt.ts` has no worker leg (single process, no `workerThreads`/pool anywhere in the file), and `SUPPORTED_PLANS` (`:75`) omits `18.18.0` → it fails closed on the new plan. Required at implementation:
  1. add `"18.18.0"` to `SUPPORTED_PLANS` (`:75`);
  2. new arm `mustFail` for 18.18.0 = `["b-pooled-yield-items","b2-pooled-yield-items-roster-unchanged","g-default-prompt-setting-drift"]`, and extend scenario `g` (currently 18.12.6/18.16.0 only, `:637`) to 18.18.0 — because 18.18.0's `heldIrcRoster` now keeps `a1`/`a2` green **without** the patch (that is the negative control proving the adopted part: mutation `a` on pristine 18.18.0 must fail only `b/b2/g`, not `a1/a2`);
  3. add a **worker leg**: inject `setSubagentWorkerPoolForTests(...)` (`thread/subagent-worker.ts:612-615`, honored at `:562`) or set `task.workerThreads: 4` and force placement, then rerun `a1,b,b2,d` and assert the revived session is worker-homed (`AgentSessionHandle.home !== main`) plus byte-identical prompt/tool schema, unchanged `session_init` count, and a single revived model request. That leg is the one that catches "worker-local state read as empty" (silent cost), which the current single-process run cannot.
  4. keep hunk #5's cosmetic blank line out.

Non-goals confirmed: capability 0101 is *not* retired; nothing else in the tree depends on its patch lines (single family, single file, `patches/.18.16.0-families/plan.tsv` root AGENT).

## 3. Capability classification
**0010 — KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL (with one small semantic merge in hunk D), effort M.**
- Not ADOPT-CANDIDATE: consumer proof is impossible — `patches/cron-extension.ts:316` (`ctx.runIdleEphemeralTurn`) and `verify-session-cache-keepalive.ts:151` have no upstream seam; the only upstream side-turn API is the unguarded `runEphemeralTurn` (`agent-session.ts:13169`). - Rebase method: re-insert A/B/C/E by hand at the anchors above; for D adopt upstream's generation body and add the 2nd parameter to both preflight helpers (no local `const generation`), then re-diff against the repaired tree. Host wiring (`runtime-init.ts:149`, `acp-agent.ts:3111`, `extension-ui-controller.ts:281,515`, `agent-session.ts:9509`) and the runner (`runner.ts:547,882,1757`) apply untouched — upstream still has exactly those 6 host/runner sites and no seventh.
- Owning gates: `verify-idle-ephemeral-reservation.ts` (apply.sh:526, unconditional) and `verify-session-cache-keepalive.ts` (apply.sh:586). Gate edits required: add `"18.18.0"` to the version whitelist and `hasSubmissionWindow` list (`verify-idle-ephemeral-reservation.ts:34,37`) and the `authStorage.keys.setRuntime` list (`:56`); 18.18.0 should also join the `["18.10.0","18.10.1"]` transition-drain legs (`:416,531`) because upstream still awaits `#idleEphemeralReservation?.settled` in every transition entry.
- Gate gap to close while rebasing: the gate drives a bare `AgentSession`, so it does not prove the 5 extension-host constructions supply the action. Add the spec's host-completeness assertion (`.trellis/spec/backend/extension-runtime-contracts.md:77-79`): `getSystemPrompt: () =>` objects must each carry `runIdleEphemeralTurn`.

## 4. Upstream 18.16.0→18.18.0 changes near the owned paths that can break the invariant even when hunks apply
1. **Preflight generation (Medium)** — 18.18.0:7046-7062, interacts with hunk D; our `#runUsageAwarePreflightForNextModelCall` now needs `setupAbort.signal` because upstream added `#usagePreflightAbortControllers` (`:1280`, registered `:7079`, removed `:7099`, aborted from `abort()` at `:11675`). Gate leg: `verify-idle-ephemeral-reservation.ts:586-646` (stale setup must never `agent.continue`).
2. **Double-abort of usage preflights (Low)** — our `navigateTree` hunk adds `for (const controller of this.#usagePreflightAbortControllers) controller.abort()`; upstream already aborts the same set in `abort()` (`:11675`). Idempotent; confirm the tree-navigation leg still observes exactly one skip (`:629-645`).
3. **Idle maintenance now also runs in a worker and persists recaps (High if E is done half-way)** — `#isQuiet` (`idle-maintenance.ts:172-180`) and `#isSettled` (`:187-189`) are both consumed by recap and idle compaction (`:316-318`, `:282-284`), and 18.18.0 rebuilt the recap lifecycle (`CHANGELOG.md:79`). Guarding only one lets an idle recap overlap the keepalive: two billed requests and a cache-thrashing side turn. Gates: `verify-idle-ephemeral-reservation.ts`, `verify-session-cache-keepalive.ts`.
4. **`runEphemeralTurn` now fails closed on model/session change (Low)** — `assertEphemeralTurnReady` throws on `this.model !== model` / `#sessionGeneration` change (`agent-session.ts:13176-13187`). Our reservation maps only *reservation* aborts to `undefined`, so a model swap mid-flight surfaces as an exception; the keepalive records `provider-error` and burns one attempt (`cron-extension.ts:283-289` preflights the model). Gate: `verify-session-cache-keepalive.ts:380-387`.
5. **Session-transition internals reshuffled (Medium)** — `#branchSummaryAbortController` creation moved into `navigateTree` (`:14334`), usage preflights abort in `abort()` (`:11675`), `#beginSessionTransition` still invalidates idle maintenance (`:6280`). Our transition-entry abort must stay before the first await of `#beginSessionTransition` (patch hunk at old `@@ -6027`); re-verify. Gate: `:406-456`.
6. **No cache-prefix/OAuth surface** — 0010 never rewrites system prompt, tools or message bytes; the side request is upstream `runEphemeralTurn` (`:13169+`). The 18.17.0 Codex `textVerbosity` default change (`CHANGELOG.md:130`) reaches main and side requests through the same `prepareSimpleStreamOptions`, so byte parity holds; the keepalive gate currently never compares the two request bodies — worth adding.

## 5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads` > 0)
**No — the capability holds no process-global or main-thread-only state.**
- Reservation, epoch and counters are instance fields (`agent-session.ts:1363-1368`); every gate predicate is a session method: `isStreaming`, `hasAdmittedSubmission` (`:3613`), `isSessionTransitioning` (`:6268`), `queuedMessageCount`, `#idleMaintenance.isRecapRunning` (patch adds the getter). A session is homed where it runs (`task/executor.ts:4377-4385`), so the main-turn entries that abort/await the reservation (`#promptWithMessage`, `#wakeForIrc`, `#scheduleAgentContinue`, `injectIdle`, transitions) execute in the reservation's thread.
- Extensions are rebound per subagent session inside the worker (`task/executor.ts:557-563`, `initializeExtensions` at `:5217`), and the keepalive's state is a per-factory closure (`patches/cron-extension.ts:159-160`), so enabling `/cron` in the main session arms nothing in workers. The worker's `AsyncLocalStorage` blocker (`runner.ts:549`) is a separate isolate instance.
- Idle maintenance on a worker-hosted session is driven from main over RPC (`thread/remote-agent-session.ts:804-816`) but its predicate is evaluated inside the worker against the same local `AgentSession`, so `hasIdleEphemeralReservation` is never read cross-thread; `RemoteAgentSession`/`AgentSessionHandle` expose only `runEphemeralTurn` (`remote-agent-session.ts:792`, `agent-session-handle.ts:393`) — no new RPC method needed.
- Failure mode with `workerThreads>0`: none for the invariant (no crash, no silent cache break). The only cross-thread behaviour is legitimate: main and subagent sessions can each hold their own reservation in different threads and issue two billed side requests under different `promptCacheKey`s.
- Gate leg that would catch a regression: `verify-idle-ephemeral-reservation.ts:129-135` (side-settled-before-main-dispatch) and `:266-269` (tree-entry refusal) are main-thread only. Add a worker leg: spawn a subagent with `task.workerThreads: 1`, have the child extension call `ctx.runIdleEphemeralTurn` while the child streams, assert `undefined` plus zero provider calls — that catches "no crash, but a duplicate request was billed".

## Files
- `patches/.18.16.0-families/0010-omp-extension-idle-ephemeral-turn-v18.16.0.patch` — failing hunks at patch lines 45-60 (A), 253-260 (B), 353-367 (C), 603-637 (D), 990-1008 (E).

# family-antigravity-wire (root AI; capabilities 0014, 0054)

## 1. Family / fuzz-0 status
- Patch: `patches/.18.16.0-families/family-antigravity-wire-v18.16.0.patch`, sha256 `84b0c5e7…e641`, single owned path `pi-ai/src/providers/google-gemini-cli.ts` (`patches/.18.16.0-families/manifest.json`).
- Replay on 18.18.0: **0 failing hunks** (`/home/chris/.cache/omp-plan-18180/replay-logs/family-antigravity-wire.txt:1` — only `patching file src/providers/google-gemini-cli.ts`, no `FAILED at`). All 5 hunks land at **offset 0** (hunk headers `-1105/-1158/-1267/-1466` match the 18.18.0 file).
- Whole file shows **no observable 18.16.0→18.18.0 drift**: every probed anchor sits on the same line in both pristine trees (`isPlanningLeakObject` :105, `ANTIGRAVITY_PROVIDER_SESSION_STATE_KEY` :304, `getAntigravityProviderSessionState` :306, `if (providerState)` :1108, `const requestId = \`agent/` :1277, `buildRequest` :1294, `requestType?: string` :448, `sessionId ?? deriveAntigravitySessionId` :1275); both files are 1472 lines. [INFERENCE] the file is byte-identical across the interval.

## 2. Failing hunks
None. The only "cause" note belongs to capability 0054, whose patch has **no hunk in the family patch at all**:
- cause = **upstream already does it** — the antigravity `buildRequest` return has no `requestType` field in either pristine tree: 18.18.0 `google-gemini-cli.ts:1457-1463` (`project/requestId/request/model/userAgent` only) and the identical block at 18.16.0:1457-1463. `requestType?: string` exists only as a dead optional type field (`:448`), and the only assignments in the whole 18.18.0 cache are Devin (`pi-ai/src/providers/devin.ts:694`) and Codex (`openai-codex-responses.ts:1835`); no antigravity header path carries it either. `patches/0054-omp-antigravity-drop-requesttype-agent.patch` (a-side 18.1.14) is stale and would no longer apply anywhere.

## 3. Per capability

| Cap | Class | Method | Gate | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0014 | **KEEP-REBASE-MECHANICAL** | Nothing to change: 18.18.0 is unchanged in this file, so the family patch is re-applied verbatim (offset 0). It keeps upstream's own `deriveSignedDecimalFromHash`/`deriveAntigravitySessionId` (`:1162`, `:1210`) and adds `deriveDeterministicUuid` + `sessionKeyOverride` + the `:side:` guards, closing upstream's remaining `randomUUID()` seeds (`:1267-1268`, `:1275` — 18.18.0 still randomizes `agentId`/`trajectoryId`/`sessionId` per process). | `verify-antigravity-envelope.ts` (wired at `patches/apply.sh:493`) | S |
| 0054 | **ADOPT-CANDIDATE** | Retire the patch file; do not carry a hunk. Consumer proof: no antigravity code path emits `requestType` in 18.18.0 (`:1457-1463`; type field :448 unused for this provider; no header equivalent in `pi-catalog/src/wire/gemini-headers.ts`). Negative control that must stay: `verify-antigravity-envelope.ts:42` `assert.equal(req1.requestType, undefined, "0054: requestType must be absent")` — it fails the moment upstream (or a patch) reintroduces the field, so the retirement is guarded without any patch. | `verify-antigravity-envelope.ts` leg 1 (`:40-42`) | S |

Notes: nothing else in the family depends on 0054. The retirement only touches `patches/0054-*.patch` + its entry in the apply list (`apply.sh:101`) — the gate leg and the family patch's determinism legs are unaffected.

## 4. Upstream changes near the owned paths that could break the invariant
1. **The provider file itself did not move** (see §1) — no drift in the request envelope, `accept-language`/labels, or `request.sessionId` bytes that carry the cache prefix.
2. **`StreamOptions.sessionId` / `promptCacheKey` semantics unchanged** (`pi-ai/src/types.ts:582`, `:588` same lines in both trees) — the 0014 seed source is intact. Severity: none.
3. **`ProviderSessionState.releaseSession?(sessionId)` is new** (`types.ts:414-415`, used at `agent-session.ts:13333` as `state.releaseSession?.(sideSessionId)`). Optional call → our antigravity state (no `releaseSession`) is simply skipped: no crash, no behaviour change. Severity: none; a future per-side-session cleanup for antigravity would need implementing it.
4. **Worker-thread subagents (18.17.0)** are the real change: a subagent's `AgentSession` is now constructed *inside* the worker (`thread/worker-warm-up.ts:47` `createAgentSession`) with its own `#providerSessionState` Map (`session/agent-session.ts:1442`). Provider identity stays stable because `sdk.ts:2137` uses `options.providerSessionId ?? sessionManager.getSessionId()` and the session file is restored on revive — i.e. the 0014 seed (`sessionId`) survives relocation, while all in-memory antigravity state does not. Severity: **medium** for a patch-less build (random ids on every park/revive), **low** with 0014 (ids re-derive identically); see §5.
5. **`:side:` convention unchanged** (`agent-session.ts:13255-13257`, identical to 18.16.0:12855-12857) and now also used by `session/session-handoff.ts:164`. Our `replace(/:side:.*$/, "")` covers both new producers; no drift.

## 5. Worker-thread assumption (workerThreads>0)
**No** — the capability reads no process-global/main-thread state. It consumes only `options.providerSessionState` (a per-`AgentSession` `Map`, `agent-session.ts:1442`) and `options.sessionId` (thread-local, `sdk.ts:4852`). The constant key `"google-antigravity-session-state"` (`google-gemini-cli.ts:304`) is only a hazard across threads if two threads shared *one* Map; workers each own their Map, so there is no cross-thread collision. `node:crypto` `createHash` used by the patch is worker-safe.

Concrete failure mode with workerThreads>0 — **silent cost, never a crash**: every park/revive of a worker-homed subagent resets `agentId/trajectoryId/sessionId/lastExecutionId/stepIndex/requestCount`. With 0014 in place the three ids re-derive from the restored `providerSessionId`, so routing/cache identity holds; what is *lost* is only `stepIndex`/`requestCount`, so `labels.request_id` restarts at `<trajectoryId>-0` and `labels.last_step_index` jumps per `max(contextSteps, …)` (`:1274`, `:1284`). Without 0014 the same reset forks to fresh random ids → full Antigravity cache miss after each relocation (the 320k-token miss 0014 exists to prevent). If a future refactor ever mints a fresh provider session id on revive, 0014 silently stops helping — also silent cost, no error.

Gate legs: `verify-antigravity-envelope.ts:44-46` (two independent `buildRequest` calls with the same `sessionId` must yield the same deterministic `sessionId`) is exactly the restart/relocation invariant, and `:48-51` pins the `:side:` sharing. Missing leg worth adding for the worker target: assert the revived subagent's `options.sessionId` equals the pre-park one (task-level, no network) — that is the one failure mode no current gate can see. Design intent: keep workerThreads at the upstream default 4; this family needs no `0` fallback.

  need re-offsetting.
- gates: `verify-hindsight-recall-promotion-slot.ts` — also **fails closed** (version list line 56);
  its `instructionArgs` must keep the 5-arg 18.12.6/18.16.0 branch (line 72) for 18.18.0.
- effort: **S** code, **M** because of the ordering proof (gate leg at line 753 "no source promotion
  may survive" is exactly what the reordering threatens).

Nothing here is ADOPT-CANDIDATE: retiring either needs a pristine 18.18.0 consumer proof (resume
replays identical recall bytes with no `memory_recall`/`hindsight_*` marker; developer message stays
2 system parts) plus a red negative control that deletes the carrier. Neither can be produced —
backend.ts:96-104 still appends the snippet and there is no promotion tail (grep `promotionTail`
hits only our patch).

## 4. Upstream changes near the owned paths that can break the invariant even when hunks apply
1. **`HindsightSessionStateApi` + `RemoteHindsightSessionState`** (state.ts:49-83, thread/services/memory-remote.ts:170-199): `aliasOf` is now a remote proxy whose `lastRecallSnippet`/`mentalModelsSnippet` are `callSync` reads of **main's live values** (memory-remote.ts:190-200). A worker subagent's developer instructions therefore embed main's current `<mental_models>` block (backend.ts:100-104). Rebase must keep the widened type or backend.ts:44-56 stops compiling.
2. **Session-switch reorder** (agent-session.ts:13766-13818): base-prompt refresh now precedes model/thinking restore; this is the ordering hazard in 0075's hunk #14 above.
3. **Outbox/retain queue** (state.ts:285, 706; backend.ts:310-319): `retainQueue.flush()`/`dispose()` are new members; the 0034 `dispose` hunk applied but must not land above them.
4. **Mental-model render-budget fix** (CHANGELOG.md:188, #17955): `tryLoadMentalModelsBlock` now keeps the last model; a transcript that replayed a frozen MM marker keeps the old bytes by design — expected, but the gate's `snapshot || undefined` legs (507-577) still hold because they drive the client, not the renderer.
5. `refreshBaseSystemPrompt(commitIf?)` signature is unchanged (agent-session.ts:7533-7535, session-tools.ts:2305), so 0034's mandatory-publication await path (state.ts:507-509 area) stays valid.

## 5. Worker-thread assumption (`task.workerThreads`, default 4)
**No module/process-global state of our own.** Every piece this family adds is per-AgentSession:
`#transcriptReadOnly`, `#recallGeneration`, `#mentalModelsLoadGeneration` on `HindsightSessionState`
(state.ts:243-285), `#baseSystemPromptPromotionTail` on `SessionMemory` (session-memory.ts:172-206),
`#memoryTranscriptReadOnly` on `AgentSession` (agent-session.ts:1462 area). No timers, no extension
runner, no auth store, no session registry.

**Alias path is closed in a worker**: a taskDepth>0 subagent builds its own concrete
`HindsightSessionState` with `aliasOf = Remote…` (backend.ts:43-56) and `hasRecalledForFirstTurn:
true`, so `beforeAgentStartPrompt` returns `undefined`; `#resetHindsightConversationTrackingIfHindsight`
returns false on `state.aliasOf` (session-memory.ts:234), so no reload/persist runs cross-thread. Any
marker it did write goes to the worker's own `SessionManager` (real `open`/`forkFrom`/`inMemory` —
executor.ts:4813-4827), never to main's branch.

**One inherited cross-thread hazard (upstream, not ours)** — answer "unclear for the invariant": the
alias's `mentalModelsSnippet` is main's live value (memory-remote.ts:198-200). With
`workerThreads>0`, a `/memory mm reload` (or a main transcript boundary) on main changes every live
subagent's developer instructions at block 3 → the subagent's provider prefix misses from that block
on (silent cost, **not** a crash), and the `callSync` blocks the worker while main is busy.
Failure mode: silent cache loss / worker stall.
**Gate leg to add** (both gates are single-process today): open one `task` with `workerThreads=4`,
snapshot the subagent's `session_init.systemPrompt`, reload MM on main, and assert (a) the subagent's
next request system blocks are byte-identical, (b) zero `hindsight_recall_snippet` /
`hindsight_mental_models_snippet` entries appear on the parent branch, (c) the parent's own promotion
tail is still exactly one part. A leg that only asserts "no exception" would be red-free and useless.

## Environment safety
Read-only: only `read`/`grep`/`glob` over pristine trees and the patch repo; no omp run, no `~/.omp`,
no DB/credentials, no patch application (the fuzz-0 counts come from the pre-existing
replay-logs/family-hindsight.txt, cross-checked against the corrected baseline for 6 hunks).
